Be composed only of income of the A/R, such as pension, RSDI, VA, accumulated interest, etc.\n\n• Be irrevocable. A revocable QIT does not meet the criteria of a QIT and will be treated as a\nresource and other rules pertaining to OBRA '93 trusts. Refer to Section 2337 - Trust Property -\nOBRA '93.\n\n• Contain no resources.\n\n。 Money from an existing account cannot be placed in the trust or\n\n。 A non-liquid resource may not be placed in the trust or\n\n。 A non-liquid resource may not be converted to a liquid resource and placed in the trust\n\n. Provide that at the A/R's death, the remainder of the trust will go to the Department of Commu-\nnity Health (DCH), up to the amount that was spent for the A/R's cost of care by Medicaid.\n\n. The QIT may NOT be backdated. It is effective beginning the month in which it is completed and\nsigned by all required parties, not before.\n\n. An A/R must establish a QIT in Georgia. A QIT established in another state cannot be used to\nqualify for Georgia Medicaid.\n\n\n### Who May Establish a QIT?\n\nThe following people may establish a QIT:\n\n. The A/R\n\n. His/her court appointed guardian or court appointed conservator\n\n. Attorney-in-fact if Power of Attorney gives that authority\n\nNOTE\nAnyone other than the A/R who establishes the QIT must present evidence of a\nPOA, court appointed Guardianship or Conservator.\n\n\n#### NOTE\n\n\n#### The NH may serve as trustee but cannot establish a trust.\n\nNOTE\n\nA QIT may not be established for a couple, only for an individual. Therefore, for\ncouples in the same LA-D, if one or both members of the couple are over the indi-\nvidual Cap and also together, they are over the Couple Cap, a QIT must be done on\nthe member(s) whose income exceeds the Individual Cap.\n\n<!-- PageFooter: Qualified Income Trust | 487 -->\n<!-- PageBreak00718 -->\n\n\n### Treatment of Income\n\n. Income placed in a QIT is NOT counted in determining income eligibility.\n\n. The income cannot be placed into the QIT by direct deposit from the source of income, i.e.,\nSocial Security, etc. However, the current month's income may be transferred from another\nbank account to the QIT account.\n\n. Income placed in a QIT AND income not placed in the QIT is counted in determining the A/R's\npatient liability/cost share.\n\n. All of the A/R's income may be placed in the QIT or just the excess over the Medicaid Cap. It is\nrecommended that all of the A/R's income be placed in the trust, if possible.\n\n. Failure to properly and timely fund the QIT will result in a loss of eligibility for that month or\nthe first month in which timely notice may be given. Properly funded simply means that you\nhave deposited, at minimum, the difference between the specified state income cap (minus a\ndollar) and the applicant's total income.\n\n. Payment for the A/R\'s medical care (i.e., PL/CS) must be paid by the end of the month following\nthe month, the income is received. If payments are not made, the A/R is not following the provi-\nsions of the trust and is not eligible for Medicaid benefits. Take action to close the Medicaid case\nfor the first month that timely notice permits. If payment is subsequently made prior to the\nexpiration of the timely notice, Medicaid eligibility may continue without interruption.\n\n. Other payments from the QIT (i.e., PNA, diversion) must be paid by the end of the month follow-\ning the month in which it is received.\n\n. Income retained by the A/R and not placed in the QIT is income to the A/R and is counted in\ndetermining income eligibility and PL/CS determination.\n\n. Income may be diverted from the QIT or from retained income to the community spouse and/or\nto dependent child(ren) according to spousal impoverishment and diversion rules. Refer to Sec-\ntion 2554 - Diversion of Income.\n\n. All the rules pertaining to allowable deductions from the PL/CS determination still apply. Refer\nto Section 2552 - Patient Liability/Cost Share Deductions. If A/R has self-employment income\nonly the net earnings from self-employment (NESE) should be placed in the QIT since expenses\nfor operating a business are not acceptable disbursements from the QIT. Refer to Section 2415 -\nSelf-Employment. The only income removed from the QIT should be for payment of the PL/CS to\nthe facility, the Personal Needs Allowance (PNA), diversion to the community spouse/dependent\nchild(ren), medical expenses of the community spouse or other medical expenses of the A/R not\ncovered by Medicaid. See "Rules for Funds Entering, Leaving and Remaining in a QIT" below.\n\n. Any other payments made from the QIT may count as income to the A/R. Payments made from\nthe QIT for medical purposes are acceptable disbursements as long as such payments do not\nnegatively impact the PL/CS payment. However, if such income was already used for the PL/CS,\nit will not be considered income a second time.\n\n\n### Rules for Funds Entering, Leaving and Remaining in a QIT\n\nIncome placed in a QIT is exempt in determining income eligibility for Medicaid. However, funds\nentering and leaving the QIT are not necessarily exempt from treatment for Medicaid.\n\n. Transfer of assets penalties do not apply to income placed in a QIT to the extent that the trust\n\n<!-- PageFooter: 488 | Qualified Income Trust -->\n<!-- PageBreak00719 -->\n\ninstrument provides that income placed in the trust will be paid out of the trust for medical care\nprovided to the A/R, including nursing home care, EWDP/CCSP care and institutionalized hos-\npice care, etc. When such payments are made, the individual is considered to have received fair\nmarket value for the income placed in the trust, up to the amount of the PL/CS.\n\n. When income remains in the QIT after the amount paid out of the trust for medical services or\nother items or services which benefit the A/R, the excess income is subject to the transfer of\nassets penalty (i.e., remaining income exceeds the Medicaid monthly billing rate of the facility).\nRefer to Section 2342 - Transfer of Assets and Appendix A1 - ABD Financial Limits.\n\n. Any income remaining in the trust that is not paid out for the A/R\'s medical care is held there to\nbe recouped by DCH at the A/R\'s death. The remainder income is not counted as a resource.\n\n. Excess income (income above the PL/CS, diversion, and PNA) placed in a QIT may be transferred\nfor the sole benefit of a spouse without incurring a penalty. Refer to Section 2502 - Deeming\n(ABD), Chart 2502.1, for a definition of "sole benefit of". This may include payments by the QIT\nfor medical care for the community spouse. There is no transfer of assets penalty for assets\ntransferred to a spouse or to a third party for the sole benefit of the spouse. To be exempt, the\nQIT instrument must be drafted to require that this particular property can be used only for the\nbenefit of the A/R\'s spouse while the QIT exists and that the QIT cannot be terminated and dis-\ntributed to any other individual or entities for any other purpose.\n\n• Inappropriate payments made from the trust (i.e., lawyer fees, mortgage payments, costs of\ndoing business, etc.) will invalidate the trust. Consider such payments as income to the A/R. The\nA/R thus becomes ineligible because the guidelines of the trust are not being followed. Such pay-\nments could only be made from non-QIT income if any remains after required payments.\n\n. The amount of income deposited into the QIT may change as long as the amount of monthly\nincome that does not go into the QIT does not equal or exceed the Medicaid Cap.\n\n\n### Who May/May Not Be the Trustee?\n\n. Spouse may serve as trustee.\n\n. Anyone named by the QIT who is willing and capable to act in that position may serve as\ntrustee.\n\n. NH may serve as trustee.\n\n. The A/R may NOT act as his/her own trustee.\n\n\n### QIT Account\n\nThe QIT account should be a banking instrument from which funds can be paid out by the trustee,\nthis could include:\n\n. Checking accounts, money market accounts, etc. (preferably non-interest bearing and no fees\nattached)\n\n. NH patient fund account if such account meets the guidelines of the QIT (composed only of\nincome, irrevocable, no resources and remainder to go to DCH), or\n\n. Banking account specifically for purpose of QIT (no commingled funds).\n\n<!-- PageFooter: Qualified Income Trust | 489 -->\n<!-- PageBreak00720 -->\n\n\n# Procedures\n\nFollow the steps below to determine the treatment of a QIT:\n\nStep 1\nGive every A/R or authorized representative of an A/R whose income exceeds the\nMedicaid Cap the following handouts found in Appendix F - TOC:\n\n. [Form 948 - QIT Approved Template 1](https://pamms.dhs.ga.gov/dfcs/medicaid/_attachments/form-948.pdf)\n\nNOTE\nIt is not a requirement for an attorney to set up or certify a QIT.\nBut if an attorney sets up the QIT the attorney should certify\nthe QIT with their State Bar Number.\n\nStep 2\nIf a QIT is established, obtain a copy of the QIT document, and compare to the [Form](https://pamms.dhs.ga.gov/dfcs/medicaid/_attachments/form-948.pdf)\n[948 QIT Approved Template 1](https://pamms.dhs.ga.gov/dfcs/medicaid/_attachments/form-948.pdf).\n\n. To be exempt, the\nQIT instrument must be drafted to require that this particular property can be used only for the\nbenefit of the A/R\'s spouse while the QIT exists and that the QIT cannot be terminated and dis-\ntributed to any other individual or entities for any other purpose.\n\n• Inappropriate payments made from the trust (i.e., lawyer fees, mortgage payments, costs of\ndoing business, etc.) will invalidate the trust. 2026-06-05T16:57:17.141339339Z [2026-06-05 16:57:17,140] [DEBUG] openai._base_client: Request options: {'method': 'post', 'url': '/deployments/text-embedding/embeddings', 'headers': {'Authorization': '<redacted>'}, 'files': None, 'post_parser': <function Embeddings.create.<locals>.parser at 0x796be468e0c0>, 'json_data': {'input': '# Rental Income\n\n\n![OF CONSTITUTION 1776](figures/201.1)\n\n\nGeorgia Division of Family and Children Services\nMedicaid Policy Manual\n\nPolicy Title:\n\nRental Income\n\nEffective Date:\n\nFebruary 2020\n\nChapter:\n\n2400\n\nPolicy Number:\n\n2410\n\nPrevious MT Num-\nber(s):\n\nMT 38\n\nUpdated or Reviewed\nin MT:\n\nMT-58\n\n\n## Requirements\n\nAny rental payment which an A/R receives for the use of real or personal property, such as land,\nhousing or machinery is included as income to the A/R in the Medicaid financial eligibility and\npatient liability/cost share budget.\n\n\n## Basic Considerations\n\nNet rental income is gross rent less the ordinary and necessary expenses paid in the same fed-\neral/state income tax year.\n\nOrdinary and necessary expenses are those necessary for the production or collection of rental\nincome. These expenses include the following:\n\n. interest on debts\n\n. state and local taxes on real and personal property and on motor fuel\n\n. general sales taxes\n\n. expenses of managing or maintaining property.\n\nNet rental income is unearned income unless it is earned income from the self-employment of\nsomeone who is in the business of renting properties.\n\n<!-- PageFooter: Rental Income | 493 -->\n<!-- PageBreak00724 -->\n\n\n# Procedures\n\n\n## Deductible Expenses\n\nDeduct the following ordinary and necessary expenses as expenses for the month they are paid:\n\n. the interest and escrow portions of a mortgage payment at the point the payment is made to the\nmortgage holder\n\n. repairs such as minor corrections to an existing structure\n\n. property taxes\n\n. fire and hazard insurance\n\n. lawn care\n\n. snow removal\n\n. advertising for tenants.\n\n\n## Nondeductible Expenses\n\nDo not deduct the following expenses from rental income:\n\n. the principal portion of a mortgage payment\n\n. a capital expenditure, which is an expense for an addition to or increase in the value of the\nproperty\n\n. the property depreciation amount claimed as a federal income tax deduction.\n\n\n## Determining Net Rental Income\n\nFollow the steps below to determine net rental income to include in the eligibility and patient liabil-\nity/cost share budgets.\n\nStep 1\nDetermine the gross rent received.\n\nStep 2\nObtain statements from two reliable sources, such as realtors, as to the Current Mar-\nket Value (CMV) for the rental of a comparable property. Use the higher of the two\namounts.\n\nStep 3\nIf the property is leased to an individual/business and is subsequently sub-leased for\na higher amount, count the higher amount of the lease payment as income to the A/R.\n\nStep 4\nDetermine the deductible expenses paid each month.\n\nStep 5\nSubtract the deductible expenses from the gross rent to determine the net rental\nincome.\n\nIf the expenses exceed the gross rent for a month, deduct the excess expenses as fol-\nlows:\n\n. Subtract the excess expenses from the next month\'s gross rent.\n\n<!-- PageFooter: 494 | Rental Income -->\n<!-- PageBreak00725 -->\n\n. Continue to subtract the excess expenses from each month\'s gross rent through\nthe end of the tax year.\n\nIf excess expenses remain after subtracting expenses through the end of the tax year,\ndeduct the remaining excess expenses as follows:\n\n. Subtract the excess expenses from the gross rent received in the month prior to\nthe month the expenses were paid.\n\n. Continue subtracting the excess expenses from each month\'s gross rent as neces-\nsary back to the beginning of the tax year until they are exhausted.\n\n\n# Verification\n\nUse documents in the individual\'s possession such as bills, receipts, etc., to verify the gross rent and\nthe dates received, and the expenses and the dates paid.\n\nNOTE\nThe individual\'s most recent federal tax return including Schedule E is helpful in\nidentifying past expenses and in estimating future rental income.\n\nIf documents are not available, obtain a signed statement from the A/R. Include an allegation of the\ngross rent and expenses paid for the period involved.\n\nNOTE\nDo not contact the tenants to verify the allegations.\n\nContact the local Internal Revenue Service (IRS) or refer to IRS Publication 527 if you are uncertain\nwhether an expense is allowable, such as whether it is an incidental repair or a capital expenditure.\nDocument the information obtained from the IRS.\n\n\n# Special Considerations\n\nApportion net rental income equally when there is more than one owner.\n\n. Split the gross rent between two joint owners before expenses are paid.\n\n. Deduct expenses paid by the A/R from his/her portion of the gross rent.\n\n. Accept a signed statement from the A/R if it explains why apportionment should not be equal.\n\nUse evidence from the retroactive period to estimate net rental income for the next 12 months.\nDeduct only predictable expenses, such as utilities, interest payments, taxes, etc.\n\nDeduct an unpredictable expense if reported in the month paid. If the expense exceeds the rent for\nthat month, recalculate the rest of the estimated period as necessary using the steps in Determining\nNet Rental Income under Procedures in this section.\n\nUse an individual\'s amortization schedule to determine the mortgage interest expense. If a sched-\nule is not available, divide the yearly interest by twelve to determine monthly interest.\n\n<!-- PageFooter: Rental Income | 495 -->\n<!-- PageBreak00726 -->\n\n\n# Self-Employment\n\n\n![CIATE OF GEORGIA CONSTITUTION 1776](figures/204.1)\n\n\n<table>\n<tr>\n<td colspan="4">Georgia Division of Family and Children Services Medicaid Policy Manual</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Policy Title:</td>\n<td colspan="3">Self-Employment</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Effective Date:</td>\n<td colspan="3">September MT:</td>\n<td>MT-73</td>\n</tr>\n</table>\n\n\n# Requirements\n\nEarnings from an applicant/recipient\'s (A/R) own business or self-employment, as opposed to wages\nor a salary from an employer, are included as earned income when determining eligibility for any\nMedicaid Class of Assistance (COA) and when determining ABD patient liability/cost share budgets.\n\n\n# Basic Considerations\n\n\n## Net Earnings\n\nThe net earnings from self-employment (NESE) is the gross income from any trade or business, plus\ncapital gains, less allowable business expenses, including depreciation.\n\nNESE also includes any distributive share (whether or not distributed) of income or loss from a\ntrade or business operated as a partnership.\n\n\n## Appreciation\n\nAppreciation, or capital gain, is an increase in the value of a business resource, and is a result of\nany of the following:\n\n. improvement in the property\n\n. normal market increases\n\n. interest accrued\n\nDetermine appreciation by obtaining verification of the value of the resources from a reliable\nsource.\n\n\n## Depreciation\n\nDepreciation occurs when a business resource loses value because of either of the following:\n\n. destruction of property in a storm, fire or other disaster\n\n. long term use of the resource reduces its value (e.g., vehicles, machinery)\n\nDetermine depreciation by obtaining verification of the value of the resource from a reliable\nsource.\n\n<!-- PageFooter: 496 | Self-Employment -->\n<!-- PageBreak00727 -->\n\n\n# Procedures\n\n\n## ABD Medicaid\n\nDevelop NESE in the following situations:\n\n. the A/R was self-employed in the prior tax year\n\n. the A/R is currently self-employed\n\n. the A/R has been self-employed during the current tax year\n\nCalculate NESE on a tax year basis.\n\n. Subtract all allowable IRS business deductions claimed on the self-employed individual\'s federal\ntax return from the gross self-employment earnings for the year to determine the NESE.\n\n. Divide the NESE equally among the 12 months in the tax year to determine monthly earnings.\n\nDivide net losses from self-employment over the tax year in the same way as net earnings. Deduct\neach month\'s net loss from other earned income for that month.\n\nDivide the entire taxable year\'s NESE equally among 12 months in the taxable year, even if the\nbusiness is seasonal, starts late in the year, ceases operation before the end of the tax year or ceases\noperation prior to the initial application for ABD Medicaid.', 'model': 'text-embedding'}}

Verify rental liability for all other living situations.\n\n\n### Public Assistance Household\n\nConsider the Medicaid individual/couple to live in a public assistance household (PA household) if\neach household member receives one of the following types of income:\n\n. Temporary Assistance for Needy Families (TANF)\n\n. Bureau of Indian Affairs general assistance programs\n\n. The Medicaid individual/couple lives in a household with at least one other person other than a\nspouse, child, or person whose income is deemed to the individual.\n\n. The individual does not have ownership interest or rental liability in the home.\n\n. The individual does not live in a PA household.\n\n. The individual, or at least one member of a couple, alleges eating no meals in the household\nduring the month.\n\n\n#### Separate Consumption of Food Verification\n\nObtain the individual's signed statement regarding separate consumption and verify the allegation\nwith a knowledgeable adult member of the household other than the individual's spouse.\n\n\n#### Separate Purchase of Food\n\nConsider a separate purchase of food exists when all of the following conditions are met:\n\n. The Medicaid individual/couple lives in a household with at least one person other than a\nspouse, child, or person whose income is deemed to the individual.\n\n. The individual does not have ownership interest or rental liability in the home.\n\n. The individual does not live in a PA household.\n\n. The individual, or both members of a couple, eats meals in the household during a month.\n\n. If another member of the household does not\nshare in the food, determine separate pro rata shares for food and shelter and add\nthem together to determine the individual's pro rata share.\n\nStep 4\nDetermine the individual's average monthly contribution toward household\nexpenses.\n\nStep 5\nCompare the contribution to the pro rata share.\n\nIf the contribution is within $5 less than or greater than the pro rata share of\nexpenses, consider the contribution and the pro rata share to be equal, and consider\nthe individual to be sharing.\n\nNOTE\nWhen computing sharing for a Medicaid couple, subtract the cou-\nple's contribution from the pro rata share of household expenses\nmultiplied by 2 to determine if sharing exists.\n\n\n## Sharing Verification\n\nObtain signed statement(s) of household expenses and contributions to establish that an individual\nis sharing.\n\n\n## Earmarked Sharing\n\nConsider the Medicaid individual/couple to be earmarked sharing when all the following conditions\nare met:\n\n. However, do not allow a $5 tolerance for earmarked sharing.\n\n<!-- PageFooter: Living Arrangement and In-Kind Support and Maintenance for ABD Medicaid | 511 -->\n<!-- PageBreak00741 -->\n\n\n### Earmarked Sharing Verification\n\nUse the verification procedures for sharing.\n\n\n### Double Earmarking\n\nWhen an individual earmarks a specific portion of his/her contribution for food and another spe-\ncific portion for shelter, it is called double earmarking.\n\nCompute the individual's pro rata share of food expenses and compare it to the portion of the con-\ntribution earmarked for food.\n\nCompute the individual's pro rata share for shelter expenses and compare it to the portion of the\ncontribution earmarked for shelter.\n\nIf either earmarked contribution equals or exceeds a pro rata share of the item for which it is ear-\nmarked, consider earmarked sharing to exist. The individual is receiving ISM in the form of the\nitem for which s/he is not earmarking. Value this ISM under the PMV rule.\n\n\n### Double Earmarking Verification\n\nIf the individual makes a contribution, verify the contribution as follows:\n\n. household.\n\nEligibility budgeting is the process in determining the A/R\'s income eligibility for ABD Medicaid by\napplying allowable deductions to the countable income of the A/R and financially responsible per-\nson.\n\n\n## Basic Considerations\n\nThe following factors must be taken into consideration in order to determine whether to consider\nthe income and resources of persons other than Refer to Appendix A for current income limits.\n\n\n# Marital Relationship\n\n\n<table>\n<tr>\n<td rowspan="5">![](figures/255.1)<br>LIATE OF GEORGIA CONSTITUTION<br>1776</td>\n<td colspan="4">Georgia Division of Family and Children Services Medicaid Policy Manual</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Policy Title:</td>\n<td colspan="3">Marital Relationship</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Effective Date:</td>\n<td colspan="3">December 2022</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Chapter:</td>\n<td>2500</td>\n<td>Policy Number:</td>\n<td>2501</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Previous MT Num- ber(s):</td>\n<td>MT 58</td>\n<td>Updated or Reviewed in MT:</td>\n<td>MT-68</td>\n</tr>\n</table>\n\n\n## Requirements\n\nThe A/R\'s marital relationship must be established to determine whether or not to consider the\nincome and resources of a person other than the A/R.\n\n\n## Basic Considerations\n\n\n### Marital Relationship (Prior to 1/1/97)\n\nA marital relationship exists for a man and a woman who are legally married or living together,\nfree to marry and holding out (common-law) to the community as married. The common-law mar-\nriage must have existed prior to 1/1/97 to be recognized as a marital relationship.\n\n<!-- PageFooter: Marital Relationship | 547 -->\n<!-- PageBreak00750 -->\n\n\n### Marital Relationship (On or after 1/1/97)\n\nA marital relationship exists for a man and a woman who are legally married. A common-law mar-\nriage established on or after 1/1/97 is not considered a marital relationship in Georgia.\n\n\n### Marital Relationships Established Prior to 1/1/97 Begins\n\nA marital relationship begins to exist the month following the month two people marry or begin liv-\ning together and holding out to the community as husband and wife.\n\nEXCEPTION\nUse of the Spousal Impoverishment resource limit is limited to situations\nwhere an A/R in LA-D has a legal spouse in the community. See Section 2502\nfor definitions of legal and non-legal spouses.\n\n\n### Marital Relationship Ends\n\nA marital relationship ceases the month following the month of separation of spouses, regardless of\nwhether or not either or both is Medicaid eligible.\n\nThe admission of one or both spouses into LA-D is considered separation.\n\nEXCEPTION\nAdmission of one spouse to a hospital is not always considered separation.\n\nRefer to Special Considerations in Couples.\n\nAn A/R is considered an individual only if a marital relationship has ended, by definition above, or\nhas never existed.\n\n\n### Procedures\n\nA marital relationship has ceased for an individual who is legally married but is living separately\nfrom his/her spouse due to estrangement (alienation, loss of affection, indifference). Non-legal mar-\nriages established on or after January 1, 1997, are not recognized as marriages in Georgia regard-\nless of the ABD Medicaid Class of Assistance (COA).\n\n\n### Legal Spouses (Prior to 1/1/97)\n\nA legal spouse is a member of a couple who has been married by legal ceremony or common-law.\nUnder Georgia law, two individuals are in a common-law marriage if they live together at least one\nnight, they are holding forth to the community as husband and wife, both are free to marry each\nother because neither is married to another person, and the relationship was established prior to\nJanuary 1, 1997. Legality matters in relationships established prior to 1/1/97 only when the A/R is in\nLA-D or applies under an ABD Medically Needy COA.\n\n<!-- PageFooter: Deeming | 549 -->\n<!-- PageBreak00752 -->\n\n\n### Legal Spouses (On or after 1/1/97)\n\nA legal spouse is a member of a couple who has been married by legal ceremony. This definition of\na legal marriage is effective as of January 1, 1997. Common-law marriages established after this\ndate are not considered legal. Legality matters in relationships established on or after 1/1/97 for all\nABD Medicaid COAs.\n\n\n### Non-Legal Spouses\n\nNon-legal spouses are holding out as husband and wife, but they are not free to marry each other.\nOne or both has a legal spouse. Otherwise known as a non-member.\n\n\n### Community Spouse\n\nA community spouse is a person who is not living in a setting that provides medical care/services,\nsuch as a medical institution or nursing facility, and who is married to:\n\n. An institutionalized person, or\n\n. A person who has been determined eligible for a Home and Community-Based Services wavier\nprogram.\n\nThe community spouse may live in his/her own home (LA-A), in a Personal Care Home/Assisted Liv-\ning (not A/R in LA-D COA) or in someone else\'s home (LA-B). If the A/R in LA-D can only be consid-\nered under the AMN COA due to income over the Medicaid Cap (no Qualified Income Trust), the\nspouse at home does not meet the definition of a Community Spouse, and therefore Spousal Impov-\nerishment regulations do not apply.\n\nIf the community spouse is living in a personal care facility, check the bill to see if the spouse is\nactually living in a medical facility. If the personal care facility is billing room and board only, the\nspouse meets the definition of a community spouse. If the Medicaid child lives with the ineligible parent, a portion of the inel-\nigible parent\'s income and resources may be considered in determining the child\'s eligibility for\nABD Medicaid.\n\n\n### Deeming Begins\n\nSpouse to Spouse Deeming begins the month following the month a marital relationship begins.\nRefer to Section 2501, Marital Relationship.\n\nParent to Child Deeming begins the month after a parent(s) and child begin living together in the\nsame household (LA-A, B or C).\n\n\n### Deeming Ceases\n\nSpouse to Spouse Deeming ceases the month following the month a marital relationship ends. Refer\nto Section 2501, Marital Relationship.\n\nParent to Child Deeming ceases the month following the month parents and their children cease liv-\ning together in the same household (LA-A, B or C).\n\nAfter an A/R ceases living with the financially responsible relative, only income actually made\navailable to the A/R is considered in determining the A/R\'s financial eligibility.\n\nEXCEPTION\nIf the responsible relative is temporarily absent from the home and is\nexpected to return within the same month or the following month, consider\nthe absent relative to be living with the A/R.\n\nWhen one spouse (or child) enters LA-D, deeming of income ceases for the LA-D A/R the month of\nadmission. If the commu-\nnity spouse is an A/R under a non-LA-D COA, deeming of income and resources ceases for the com-\nmunity spouse the month following the month of admission.\n\nWhen both spouses enter LA-D, treat both A/Rs as individuals beginning the month of admission in\ndetermining income eligibility. See Section 2510-2 for exceptions.\n\nFor resource eligibility, treat both A/Rs as a couple the month of admission and as individuals the\nmonth following the month of admission.\n\nEXCEPTION\nAdmission to a hospital does not terminate the hospitalized individual\'s\nfinancial responsibility for his/her spouse or child residing in the commu-\nnity. During the application process the A/R and spouse must have resources under the\nSpousal Impoverishment Limit. The LA-D spouse cannot avoid ineligibility by transferring excess\nresources to the community spouse.\n\n\n### Prior Months Eligibility\n\nCombine the countable resources owned by the A/R and the community spouse on the first day of\nthe prior month and apply them to the Spousal Impoverishment resource limit.\n\nIf the combined resources are greater than the Spousal Impoverishment resource limit, the A/R is\nineligible based on resources. Deny Medicaid for the prior month.\n\nIf the combined resources are less than or equal to the Spousal Impoverishment resource limit, the\nA/R is eligible based on resources for the prior month.\n\n<!-- PageNumber: Deeming | 553 -->\n<!-- PageBreak00756 -->\n\n\n## Ongoing Eligibility\n\nIf the A/R meets the eligibility criteria for LOS and LOC, follow the steps below to determine the\nongoing resource eligibility for an A/R in LA-D who has a community spouse:\n\nStep 1\nCombine the countable resources owned by the A/R and the community spouse on\nthe first day of the month of application and apply them to the Spousal Impoverish-\nment resource limit. If the combined resources are greater than the Spousal Impov-\nerishment resource limit, the A/R is ineligible based on resources. Deny the applica-\ntion.\n\nIf the combined resources are less than or equal to the Spousal Impoverishment\nresource limit, the A/R is eligible based on resources. Subtract the Individual resource limit appropriate for the COA from the parent's countable\nresources.\n\n. Deem the amount in excess of the Individual resource limit to the eligible child(ren).\n\nIf two parents are living in the household with the Medicaid child, deem the parents' resources as\nfollows:\n\n. Subtract the Couple resource limit appropriate for the COA from the combined countable\nresources of both parents.\n\n. Deem the amount in excess of the Couple resource limit to the eligible child(ren).\n\nIf the Medicaid child enters LA-D, deem the parent(s)' resources for the month of admission to LA-\nD. Consider only the child's resources beginning the month following the month of admission to LA.\n\n<!-- PageFooter: 556 | Deeming -->\n<!-- PageBreak00758 -->\n\n\n## Parent to Child Deeming of Income\n\nComplete parent to child deeming of income on Form 171 if the A/R is a Medicaid child living in the\nhousehold with his/her parent(s). Expenses of obtaining income (less)\n\n. Expenses of converting payments in foreign currency to U.S. dollars (less)\n\n. Garnishment (more)\n\n. Gross earnings, before any deductions (more)\n\n. In cases where SSA requires that payments be made to a representative payee, the\nappointment of a legal guardian is unnecessary, and guardianship fees are not allowable.\n\n\n## Procedures\n\n\n### Expenses of Obtaining Income\n\nDeduct that part of a payment that is an essential expense incurred in obtaining the payment.\n\n. Subtract legal, medical and other expenses connected with an accident from the payment for\ndamages received in the accident.\n\n. Subtract legal fees connected with that claim if an individual receives a retroactive payment\nfrom a benefit program other than SSI.\n\nDeduct expenses from the first and any subsequent payments of the same type of income until all\nexpenses are eliminated.\n\n. Deduct those verified expenses which the recipient has previously paid (e.g., a partial payment\nto an attorney made from the individual\'s savings account) as long as the expenses are essen-\ntial.\n\n. Use bills, receipts, contact with the provider, etc., to verify all essential expenses.\n\n. If the individual alleges and can establish that the pay-\nment was received too late in the month for conversion, count the payment as income for the fol-\nlowing month.\n\nUse a check or documents in the individual\'s possession to verify receipt of a foreign payment and\nthe amount in foreign currency. If the payment is made directly to a bank, the bank may provide a\nstatement of the amount received.\n\nIf the evidence is not readily available, or if translation of the documents would require a delay\nbeyond the receipt of the next payment, complete the following procedures:\n\n. These deductions are not allowed in Medicaid CAP cases.\n\n\n# Basic Considerations\n\nIncome deductions apply to all earned and unearned income except Income Based on Need (IBON).\nIBON includes, but is not limited to, the following types of income:\n\n. TANF\n\n. IV-E Foster Care/Adoption Assistance\n\n• SSI\n\n. VA Pension\n\n. VA compensation only when received by a parent(s) due to his/her child\'s military related\n\n. death\n\n. Other needs based on payment from private organizations (e.g., Salvation Army, Catholic chari-\nties).\n\n\n# Procedures\n\n\n## Unearned Income Deductions\n\nSubtract a $20 general deduction from each month of unearned income. Subtract only one $20 gen-\neral deduction from the unearned income of each of the following:\n\n. a Medicaid Individual\n\n. a Medicaid Individual and his/her Ineligible Spouse\n\n. a Medicaid Couple\n\n. a Medicaid Child\n\n. an Ineligible Parent(s).\n\nSubtract one third of the amount of any child support payments received for the child A/R. Child\nsupport payments are considered as income for the child for whom the payments are intended.\n\n<!-- PageFooter: 568 | Income Deductions -->\n<!-- PageBreak00766 -->\n\n\n## Earned Income Deductions\n\nSubtract earned income deductions for each month of earned income in the following order:\n\n. Deduct up to $400 per month, but not more than $1,620 in a calendar year, of the earned\nincome of a blind or disabled child who is a student.\n\n. Subtract the remainder of the $20 general deduction from the combined earned income of the\nfollowing A/Rs and/or deemors:\n\n。 Medicaid Individual\n\no a Medicaid Couple or a Medicaid Individual with an Ineligible Spouse\n\no a Medicaid Child\n\n。 Ineligible Parents\n\n. Deduct $65 of gross earned income. Subtract the deduction from the combined earned income\nof the following A/Rs and/or deemors:\n\n。 a Medicaid Individual\n\n。 a Medicaid Couple or a Medicaid Individual with an Ineligible Spouse\n\no a Medicaid Child\n\n。 Ineligible Parents\n\n. Deduct the earned income of disabled Individuals used to pay impairment-related work\nexpenses.\n\n. Deduct one-half of any remaining earned income.\n\n. Deduct the earned income of blind Individuals used to meet work expenses. Deduct any earned\nincome used to fulfill an approved Plan to Achieve Self Sufficiency (PASS).\n\n\n## Applying Earned Income Deductions\n\nUse the following guidelines when applying the earned income deductions:\n\n. Never reduce earned income below zero\n\n. Do not apply any unused portion of an earned income deduction to unearned income.\n\n. Do not apply any unused portion of an earned income deduction in subsequent months.\n\n\n## Student Child Earned Income Deduction\n\nDeduct up to $400 per month, but not more than $1,620 in a calendar year, of the earned income of\na blind or disabled child A/R who is a student regularly attending school.\n\nGive the student earned income deduction to an Individual who meets all the following criteria:\n\n. is a child under age 22\n\n. is unmarried\n\n. is not the head of the household\n\n. is a student regularly attending (or expects to attend) school at least one month of the current\n\n<!-- PageFooter: Income Deductions | 569 -->\n<!-- PageBreak00767 -->\n\n\n### calendar quarter\n\nApply the deduction using the following guidelines:\n\n. Apply the deduction consecutively to months in which the child has earned income until the\ndeduction is exhausted or the Individual is no longer a student child.\n\n. Income placed in a Qualified Income Trust (QIT) may be used for\ndiversion to community spouse and/or dependent family member.\n\n\n# Income Diverted to a Community Spouse\n\nThe Community Spouse Maintenance Need Standard (CSMNS) is used to determine the amount of\ndiverted income if the following conditions are met:\n\n. The recipient chooses to make the allowance available to the community spouse or to someone\nelse for the benefit of the community spouse.\n\nNOTE\nIf a court has entered an order for monthly income for support of the community\nspouse by the institutionalized spouse, the income allowance for the community\nspouse shall not be less than the amount of the monthly income so ordered.\n\n\n# Income Diverted to a Dependent Family Member\n\nThe Dependent Family Member Maintenance Need Standard (DFMNS), Appendix A1 - ABD Finan-\ncial Limits TOC, Chart A1.10, is used to determine the amount diverted to a financially dependent\nfamily member who lives with the community spouse.\n\nThe family member must fall within the acceptable degree of relationship. The degree of the rela-\ntionship can be either to the recipient or the community spouse and is restricted to the following:\n\n. a minor child\n\n<!-- PageFooter: Diversion of Income | 585 -->\n<!-- PageBreak00783 -->\n\n. dependent child\n\n. dependent parent\n\n. dependent sibling, including half-sibling.\n\nThe family member must be claimed as a dependent on the most recent IRS tax return in order to\nbe considered financially dependent. Family member\'s age or disability is not a consideration when\ndetermining dependency status.\n\nIncome is diverted to a dependent family member regardless of the following factors:\n\n. whether the recipient makes the income available to the dependent family member or to some-\none else on behalf of the dependent family member.\n\n. whether income is diverted to the community spouse with whom the dependent family member\nis living.\n\n\n# Non-Legal Spouse\n\nIncome of the recipient may be diverted to a non-legal spouse/child(ren) at home to meet their\nneeds.\n\n. Use the Individual FBR for LA-A as the diversion standard for a non-legal spouse with no minor\nchildren at home.\n\n. Use the TANF standard of need (Appendix A1 - ABD Finanical Limits TOC) as the diversion stan-\ndard for a non-legal spouse with minor children at home.\n\nNOTE\nIf the spouse and/or minor children are receiving TANF or SSI, the spouse may\ndecide not to receive the diverted income to avoid possible ineligibility for SSI\nor TANF.\n\n\n## Couples Under EDWP/CCSP\n\nDiversion of income applies when both members of a Medicaid couple are in EDWP/CCSP.\n\nWhen one member of the couple (Spouse A) has income less than the individual FBR for LA-A,\ndivert the income of his/her spouse (Spouse B) as follows:\n\n. Subtract the income of Spouse A from the individual FBR for LA-A.\n\n. Divert the amount from the above calculation (deficit) to Spouse A from Spouse B\'s income.\n\n\n## Procedures\n\nObtain a Notification Requirement: [Form 129 - Transfer of Assets to Spouse,](https://pamms.dhs.ga.gov/dfcs/medicaid/_attachments/form-129.pdf) from the recipient or\nPR during the initial application process.\n\nObtain a statement from the recipient if the income being diverted is being provided to someone\nelse for the care and upkeep of the community spouse/dependent family member.\n\n<!-- PageFooter: 586 | Diversion of Income -->\n<!-- PageBreak00784 -->\n\n\n### Community Spouse\n\nDetermine the amount to be diverted using the adjusted gross income of the spouse and/or depen-\ndent family member, including In-Kind Support and Maintenance. 2026-06-05T16:58:24.095746410Z [2026-06-05 16:58:24,094] [DEBUG] openai._base_client: Request options: {'method': 'post', 'url': '/deployments/text-embedding/embeddings', 'headers': {'Authorization': '<redacted>'}, 'files': None, 'post_parser': <function Embeddings.create.<locals>.parser at 0x796be468e0c0>, 'json_data': {'input': 'Averaging and reconciliation procedures are used in all patient liability and cost share budgets.\n\nAveraging periods are three months, beginning with the month of admission to LA-D or the first\nmonth of Medicaid eligibility, whichever is later.\n\nNOTE\nAn averaging period may be shortened to avoid reconciling income at the end of a\nperiod that would cause hardship to an A/R.\n\nThe expected average income and IMEs for the ongoing three months are projected as accurately as\npossible. The projected average is based on the following:\n\n· regular recurring income/IMEs received/incurred during the three previous months\n\n· regular recurring income/IMEs expected to be received/ incurred during the ongoing three\nmonth period\n\n. any irregular or non-recurring income/IMEs expected to be received during the ongoing three-\nmonth period (e.g. lump sums, quarterly interest, a large, one-time IME).\n\nNOTE\nThe projection should not include income or IMEs received or incurred in the pre-\nvious three months if it is not expected to be received or incurred in the ongoing\nthree month period.\n\nAveraging periods can be flexible, depending on the receipt of unexpected income.\n\nA budget is completed every three months to reconcile the projected income with the actual income\nreceived.\n\nNOTE\nLarge medical expenses which are a one-time, non-recurring expense are not\ntreated in the manner of a significant change because it is not a recurring event.\nThese are to be treated separately with no averaging. See Section 2558 - 1.\n\n\n# Procedures\n\n\n## Averaging\n\nFollow the steps below to determine a monthly average income and IME:\n\nStep 1\nVerify the gross income received by the individual, including income placed in a\nQualified Income Trust (QIT), for each of the three months previous to the first\nmonth of the averaging period.\n\nStep 2\nDetermine the total gross income for each month separately. Round each monthly\ntotal down to the nearest dollar.\n\n<!-- PageFooter: Averaging Income and Incurred Medical Expenses | 593 -->\n<!-- PageBreak01306 -->\n\nStep 3\nAdd the monthly totals together and divide by three. Round down to the nearest dol-\nlar.\n\nStep 4\nDetermine if the individual expects to receive any income in the averaging period\nwhich was not received in the previous three months (e.g., yearly farm rental\nincome). Divide this total by three. Round down to the nearest dollar.\n\nStep 5\nAdd the total from Step 3 to the total from Step 4. This is the projected monthly aver-\nage income to be used in the patient liability budgets for the averaging period.\n\nStep 6\nVerify all allowable IMEs for each of the three months previous to the first month of\nthe averaging period. Refer to Incurred Medical Expenses.\n\nStep 7\nDetermine the total amount of IME for each month separately. Round each month\'s\ntotal up to the nearest dollar.\n\nStep 8\nAdd the monthly totals together and divide by three. Round up to the nearest dollar.\n\nStep 9\nDetermine if the A/R expects to incur any medical expense in the averaging period\nwhich was not incurred in the previous three months. Divide this total by three.\nRound up to the nearest dollar.\n\nStep 10\nAdd the total from Step 8 to the total from Step 9. This is the projected monthly aver-\nage IME to be used in the patient liability budgets for the averaging period.\n\nStep 11\nComplete patient liability budgets for the averaging period using the projected\nmonthly averages for income and IME from step 10.\n\n\n## Reconciliation\n\nReconcile the projected amounts of income and IME used for the averaging period with the actual\nincome/IME received.\n\nReconciliation occurs in three instances:\n\n. at the beginning of each new averaging period\n\n. whenever a significant change occurs (refer to Significant Change in Income or IME)\n\n. whenever the vendor payment or cost share is terminated (except for discharges to another\nnursing home).\n\nThe reconciliation month is the first month of a new averaging period. It must be completed in the\ncurrent benefit month in the current eligibility system, allowing for timely notice. Refer to Notifica-\ntion.\n\nThe following instructions are for completing manual reconciliation.\n\nStep 1\nReconciliation should be completed in the system answering the Apply towards rec-\nonciliation question found on the Income or Medical Expense screens.\n\nStep 2\nVerify the actual income received by the individual, including income placed in a\nQIT, for each month of the just completed three month averaging period.\n\n<!-- PageFooter: 594 | Averaging Income and Incurred Medical Expenses -->\n<!-- PageBreak01307 -->\n\nStep 3\nDetermine the total gross income for each month separately. Round each monthly\ntotal down to the nearest dollar.\n\nStep 4\nCombine the three rounded monthly totals from Step 2 to determine the total actual\nincome which should have been used in the just completed averaging period.\n\nStep 5\nCombine the three projected average monthly incomes used in the just completed\naveraging period to determine the total projected income. Compare the total pro-\njected income from Step 4 to the total amount from Step 3.\n\nIf the total actual income is greater than the total projected income, add the differ-\nence in actual income and projected income from the income for the first month of\nthe new averaging period (see Step 9).\n\nStep 6\nIf the total actual income is less than the total projected income, subtract the differ-\nence in actual income and projected income from the income for the first month of\nthe new averaging period (Step 9).\n\nRepeat Steps 1 through 4, using IMEs instead of income. Use only IMEs which have\nbeen verified timely to determine actual IME. Round each IME total up to the near-\nest dollar.\n\nStep 7\nCompare the total projected IME with the total actual IME.\n\n. If the total actual IME is greater than the total projected IME, add the difference\nin actual IME and projected IME to the IME deduction for the first month of the\nnew averaging period (see Step 9).\n\n. If the total actual IME is less than the actual projected IME, subtract the differ-\nence in actual IME and projected IME from the IME deduction for the first\nmonth of the new averaging period (see Step 9).\n\nStep 8\n\nUse the averaging procedures previously explained to determine new projected\naverages for both income and IME to use in the new averaging period.\n\nStep 9\nComplete a patient liability budget for the first month of the new averaging period.\n\n. Add the income reconciliation amount from Step 5 to the new projected average\nincome used in the patient liability budget for the first month of the new averag-\ning period.\n\n. Add the IME reconciliation amount from Step 7 to the new projected average\nIME amount used in the patient liability budget for the first month of the new\nongoing budget period.\n\nStep 10\nComplete patient liability budgets for the second and third months of the new ongo-\ning budget period using the new projected average income and IME amounts.\n\nStep 11\nComplete appropriate fields in the computer system to calculate the new patient lia-\nbility for the months of the new averaging period.
